# Foundation: what Isomer does not have yet

Every declaration of Apple's Foundation interface (iOS 27.0 SDK) that Isomer's Foundation lacks, with Apple's signature: the list to choose what to implement next from. [Foundation.md](../Foundation.md) has the counts, the reasons and what is implemented differently. Generated by `tools/parity/parity.py` on 2026-10-24 with Isomer at `318c9f2f`.

Areas are ordered planned, then maybe, then the rest, larger first; within an area, types with the most missing first. "The type too" marks a type Isomer does not declare at all; elsewhere the type is there and these members are not. A version is the iOS release that introduced the declaration.

| Area | Missing | Intent |
|---|---|---|
| [Formatting, locales, calendars and time zones](#formatting-locales-calendars-and-time-zones-1117) | 1117 | planned: what of it remains once FoundationInternationalization is in |
| [Processes, file handles and file resources](#processes-file-handles-and-file-resources-398) | 398 | planned (after 0.31) |
| [User defaults and notifications](#user-defaults-and-notifications-100) | 100 | planned (after 0.31) |
| [Streamed bodies](#streamed-bodies-31) | 31 | planned (after 0.31) |
| [Other members](#other-members-639) | 639 | planned, as apps need them |
| [Formatter classes](#formatter-classes-295) | 295 | maybe (DateFormatter and NumberFormatter as Swift classes over FormatStyle) |
| [Units and measurements](#units-and-measurements-249) | 249 | maybe |
| [Threads, run loops, timers and operations](#threads-run-loops-timers-and-operations-202) | 202 | maybe |
| [Serialization and parsing classes](#serialization-and-parsing-classes-199) | 199 | maybe |
| [Caches, cookies, credentials and URL protocols](#caches-cookies-credentials-and-url-protocols-192) | 192 | maybe |
| [URLSession delegates and metrics](#urlsession-delegates-and-metrics-115) | 115 | maybe (Swift delegate protocols whose methods have default implementations, with the `delegate:` forms) |
| [Resumable transfers](#resumable-transfers-9) | 9 | maybe |
| [URLSession's Combine publisher](#urlsessions-combine-publisher-9) | 9 | maybe (with a Combine subset) |
| [Newer than Isomer's swift-foundation](#newer-than-isomers-swift-foundation-111) | 111 | arrives when Isomer moves to the next Swift release |
| Total | 3666 | |

After the areas, what Isomer's types lack beyond declarations: conformances 138, overloads with other parameter types 383.

Most missing by type (all areas):

`Date` 166, `URL` 139, `AttributedString` 130, `Morphology` 122*, `XMLParser` 119*, `URLResourceKey` 118, `URLResourceValues` 114*, `AttributeScopes` 113, `NumberFormatter` 96*, `Measurement` 90, `ProgressManager` 90*, `PersonNameComponents` 70*, `RunLoop` 65*, `String` 61*, `NotificationCenter` 61, `PresentationIntent` 56*, `OperationQueue` 56*, `PredicateExpressions` 53, `DateFormatter` 53*, `URLSession` 51, `Locale` 49, `FileHandle` 46, `ProcessInfo` 39, `FileManager` 37, `UnitInformationStorage` 36*, `POSIXError` 35, `URLSessionTaskTransactionMetrics` 35*, `DateComponentsFormatter` 34*, `Duration` 33*, `ByteCountFormatter` 33*

(* the type itself is missing.)

## Formatting, locales, calendars and time zones (1117)

Why: Formatting and parsing (`FormatStyle`, `ParseStrategy`, `formatted()`), locale data, calendars other than the Gregorian and time zones other than GMT, measurement and person-name formatting, inflection: FoundationInternationalization, which needs ICU and is an app's choice.

Intent: planned: what of it remains once FoundationInternationalization is in.

## User defaults and notifications (100)

Why: `UserDefaults` over each platform's own preferences (Isomer's SwiftUI keeps `@AppStorage` there already), and the rest of `NotificationCenter`.

Intent: planned (after 0.31).

## Streamed bodies (31)

Why: A body read as it arrives (`bytes(for:)`, `URLSession.AsyncBytes`, `URL.lines`, `resourceBytes`, the async line and character sequences over bytes) or sent as it is produced (`uploadTask(withStreamedRequest:)`). Isomer's transports hand a body over whole (a request's `httpBodyStream` is read whole before it is sent); these come with a streaming transport (a `fetch` response's `ReadableStream`, OkHttp's source).

Intent: planned (after 0.31).

## Other members (639)

Why: Members of types Isomer's Foundation has (`Data`, `String`, `AttributedString`, `URL`, `Bundle`, `Stream`, errors, predicates, the standard library's types) that it lacks: often an overload taking an Objective-C type or a newer form.

Intent: planned, as apps need them.

## Formatter classes (295)

Why: Objective-C formatter classes (`DateFormatter`, `NumberFormatter`, `ISO8601DateFormatter`…), which `FormatStyle` replaces in Swift.

Intent: maybe (DateFormatter and NumberFormatter as Swift classes over FormatStyle).

## Threads, run loops, timers and operations (202)

Why: `Thread`, `RunLoop`, `Timer`, `Operation` and `OperationQueue`, `NotificationQueue`: Objective-C run-loop machinery that Swift concurrency and Dispatch cover in Isomer apps.

## Caches, cookies, credentials and URL protocols (192)

Why: Apple's network stack keeps a response cache, cookies and credentials of its own, and loads schemes of an app's through `URLProtocol` subclasses it registers; all of them are `NSObject` classes. Under Isomer the platform's stack keeps its own (a page's cookies and HTTP cache are the browser's), and a test installs a transport (IsomerFoundation's `IsomerNetwork`) where on Apple platforms it would register a `URLProtocol`.

Intent: maybe.

## URLSession delegates and metrics (115)

Why: Session and task delegates are Objective-C protocols (refinements of `NSObjectProtocol` with optional requirements), a session's called on its `delegateQueue`, an `OperationQueue`; the dispositions and the task metrics exist for them. Isomer's tasks report through completion handlers and the `async` forms. `data(for:)`, `upload(for:from:)`, `download(for:)` and the others are there as an app calls them without a delegate (the `delegate:` parameter's default is nil); the forms that take one are not.

Intent: maybe (Swift delegate protocols whose methods have default implementations, with the `delegate:` forms).

## Resumable transfers (9)

Why: Resume data for a download or upload that was cancelled or failed, and the tasks made from it: the platform's stack would have to keep the partial transfer and ask for the rest by range. An Isomer task that fails starts again from the beginning.

Intent: maybe.

### `URLSession` (5)

- `func download(resumeFrom resumeData: Data, delegate: (any URLSessionTaskDelegate)? = nil) async throws -> (URL, URLResponse)` (iOS 15)
- `func downloadTask(withResumeData resumeData: Data) -> URLSessionDownloadTask` (iOS 7)
- `func downloadTask(withResumeData resumeData: Data, completionHandler: @escaping @Sendable (URL?, URLResponse?, (any Error)?) -> Void) -> URLSessionDownloadTask`
- `func uploadTask(withResumeData resumeData: Data) -> URLSessionUploadTask` (iOS 17)
- `func uploadTask(withResumeData resumeData: Data, completionHandler: @escaping @Sendable (Data?, URLResponse?, (any Error)?) -> Void) -> URLSessionUploadTask` (iOS 17)

### `URLSessionDownloadTask` (2)

- `func cancel(byProducingResumeData completionHandler: @escaping @Sendable (Data?) -> Void)` (iOS 7)
- `func cancelByProducingResumeData() async -> Data?` (iOS 7)

### `URLSessionUploadTask` (2)

- `func cancel(byProducingResumeData completionHandler: @escaping @Sendable (Data?) -> Void)` (iOS 17)
- `func cancelByProducingResumeData() async -> Data?` (iOS 17)

## URLSession's Combine publisher (9)

Why: `URLSession.DataTaskPublisher` and `dataTaskPublisher(for:)` are Combine's; a `Combine` module is not part of 0.31.

Intent: maybe (with a Combine subset).

## Newer than Isomer's swift-foundation (111)

Intent: arrives when Isomer moves to the next Swift release.
